We further validate the generalization capability of FlowMoDE on realistic robotic scenes, with qualitative comparisons against state-of-the-art methods. The project page isthis https URL
  </details>

- **[MATS: A Multi-Agent Framework for Controllable and Reflective Tumor Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41159)**  `arXiv:2610.41159`  
  _Zhongnan Shi, Keyang Zhou, Siyi Li, Qichao Ying, Xinchen Ma, Shuyi Gong, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Synthesizing realistic tumors on medical images helps address data scarcity for clinical AI. Existing diffusion-based methods, however, lack fine-grained attribute-level controllability, closed-loop quality verification, and awareness of which morphological patterns are underrepresented in the target distribution. We present MATS, a multi-agent framework for text-controllable, cross-organ tumor synthesis. Given a target disease, MATS first profiles its morphological distribution over a fine-grained attribute space covering tumor geometry and appearance, and constructs a generation plan that amplifies underrepresented subtypes while keeping common ones dominant. It then localizes the target organ, proposes anatomically valid placement, and executes two-stage diffusion to produce the tumor mask and render texture, each conditioned on the corresponding attributes. A closed-loop reflective mechanism verifies attribute alignment at three checkpoints throughout synthesis and provides targeted feedback to guide re-generation when misalignment is detected. Trained exclusively on a single-source pancreatic neuroendocrine tumor dataset, MATS consistently improves downstream segmentation both in-domain and across LiTS, KiTS, and MSD-Pancreas benchmarks, whereas most prior generative augmentation methods fall below real-only training.
  </details>

- **[MotorSense: A Video-EMG Dataset of Motoric Representations for Action Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41127)**  `arXiv:2610.41127`  
  _Eadom Dessalene, Michael Maynord, Amir-Hossein Shahidzadeh, Botao He, Yasamin Tabatabaee, Ruoshi Liu, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Computer vision models learn hand action only from its visual consequences, without access to the underlying embodied motor signals. We address this absence with MotorSense, the first large-scale paired egocentric video and surface electromyography (EMG) dataset. MotorSense pairs bimanual EMG bands with egocentric video and yields synchronized video, bimanual EMG and IMU, and audio across 100 hours of unscripted manipulation. EMG offers a powerful scalable alternative to manual annotation: it captures finger movement, hand configuration, and the forces exerted between hand and environment as a dense signal free of occlusion and labeler bias, whereas manual annotation is costly, subjective, and unreliable under occlusion. We use MotorSense to learn sensorimotor alignment, producing an organization of action that is shared across the visual and motoric spaces, through cross-modal pre-training between video and EMG. The resultant vision models are then transferred to downstream tasks that benefit from sensorimotor alignment: in this case, action recognition, 3D hand reconstruction, and 3D contact reconstruction. We further demonstrate a generative application in which EMG motor signals are used to condition a video diffusion model, yielding videos of fine-grained hand manipulation. Across four action-centric computer vision tasks and eight popular video datasets, we demonstrate that sensorimotor alignment provides a stronger foundation for visual representation learning than conventional self-supervised learning methods over video, outperforming every baseline across all tasks and datasets evaluated.
  </details>

- **[CompoSET: A Controlled Single-Edit Benchmark for Vision-Language Compositionality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41118)**  `arXiv:2610.41118`  
  _Laure Delisle, Pietro Perona_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-language compositionality is the ability to identify which properties, relations, quantities, and states bind to which entities in both an image and a caption, and to match those bindings across modalities. Existing benchmarks evaluate this ability through image-text matching: a model ranks candidate image-caption match similarities and succeeds when the correct match comes on top. In such datasets, paired images differ by more than a single binding: scene, layout, and content all differ, with 97% of pixels changing on average. Such pairs do not isolate compositionality: results are confounded by global image differences. We introduce Compositional Single-EdiT (CompoSET), a benchmark dataset in which each base image-caption pair has an edited counterpart, or foil, that preserves the scene but differs by a single controlled edit, such as color, count, spatial relation, or object state. Base and foil images preserve naturalistic scene complexity yet are visually near-identical: only 8% of pixels change on average. Our dataset has many foils per base scene (~22) in order to separate scene difficulty from edit difficulty, a distinction one-foil-per-base benchmarks cannot make. CompoSET contains 1,776 instances across 80 naturalistic scenes, spanning 16 edit types in 4 semantic categories, with captions at 3 verbosity levels. We test 22 dual-encoder VLMs and find that all models perform at or below chance level on CompoSET, despite scoring well on prior benchmarks. This suggests that apparent progress under prior image-text matching protocols may be an artifact arising from confounds in benchmark design, and that compositional understanding in dual-encoder VLMs is far from solved. CompoSET addresses the confounds and offers a better controlled benchmark to measure future progress.
  </details>

- **[An Empirical Study on Learning Rooftop Solar Potential from Satellite Imagery Directly Without Explicit Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41111)**  `arXiv:2610.41111`  
  _Joshua Lee_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Rooftop solar potential estimation often relies on LiDAR, digital surface models (DSMs), or pipelines that explicitly reconstruct roof geometry before estimating solar potential. We propose Direct Rooftop Solar Prediction (DRSP), an end-to-end formulation that maps satellite imagery and coarse regional sunlight information directly to application-level solar outputs. DRSP neither requires explicit geometric representations as input nor predicts roof orientation, slope, height, DSMs, or other geometric quantities as intermediate outputs, and therefore requires no geometric intermediate supervision. To enable large-scale study of this formulation, we develop a programmatic data-curation pipeline that converts per-panel information from the Google Solar API into usable-rooftop masks and spatial solar-energy targets, yielding two geographically distinct datasets containing approximately 60K rooftops from the U.S. Midwest and 87K from Texas. We demonstrate the feasibility of DRSP and systematically characterize the effects of geographic training distribution, model depth, image resolution, regional sunlight conditioning, and heatmap regression objectives. Overall, our results suggest that useful rooftop-scale solar information can be learned directly from 2D imagery without explicit geometric intermediate representations, offering a simpler path toward large-scale solar assessment. Our code and dataset are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Grounding Image Composition with Label-Conditioned Visual Evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41080)**  `arXiv:2610.41080`  
  _Armin Dadras, Tingyu Lin, Robert Sablatnig, Franziska Proksa, Markus Seidl_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Image composition labels such as center, diagonal, or rule of thirds describe visual structure rather than object categories. KU-PCP provides image-level labels and class-specific geometric elements, but does not show which visible regions people associate with each of its nine classes. We introduce KU-PCP Elements (KU-PCPE), a label-conditioned region-evidence benchmark for photographic composition. Given an image and a label already judged applicable, the task is to segment the visible regions that explain why the label applies. KU-PCPE contains 4,093 single-annotator training masks and a 1,327-task expert test split with three masks per image-label task. The annotations also include exploratory segment-versus-line ratings that describe whether region or semantic-guideline evidence better explains the label. A held-out expert reaches .867 Dice against the other experts, while the strongest deployable model reaches .664, a proposal oracle .672, and the best vision-language model (VLM) proposal selector .395. KU-PCPE exposes the gap between recognizing a composition label and grounding the human-aligned visual evidence that makes it plausible in a particular image.
  </details>

- **[Single-Annotation, Multi-Hypothesis Segmentation with Semantic-Spatial Residual Banks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41069)**  `arXiv:2610.41069`  
  _Armin Dadras, Robert Sablatnig, Franziska Proksa, Markus Seidl_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-rater segmentation datasets show that a single image can admit several valid masks, yet most training sets expose only one annotation per image. We study single-annotation multi-hypothesis segmentation: each training image exposes one fixed mask, while the model must return a finite bank that covers held-out human annotations at test time. Our experiments reveal a representation tradeoff. Banks learned directly from images recover variations in local boundaries and extent but lose structural hypotheses, whereas frozen semantic features preserve structural grouping but underrepresent spatial variation. We introduce the Semantic-Spatial Residual Bank (SSRB), which freezes a semantic bank trained with balanced assignments and learns a residual from the image for each hypothesis. Across five medical endpoints, SSRB improves coverage of held-out masks at $K=8$ over a Probabilistic U-Net under the same one-mask constraint. On LIDC and LIDC-hard, it also produces substantially more distinct useful masks. On BSDS, it preserves the semantic bank's performance in grouping regions. Its advantage grows as more masks are returned, showing that residuals learned for corresponding heads improve coverage of local annotation variation while preserving how regions are grouped.
  </details>

- **[Segment Anything Pancreas: A Unified MRI Segmentation Model for Pancreatic Cancer Radiotherapy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41063)**  `arXiv:2610.41063`  
  _Zach Eidex, Mojtaba Safari, Kundan Thind, Parag J. Parikh, Zhen Tian, Xiaofeng Yang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Background: Limited tumor annotations and incomplete labels across MRI datasets hinder unified segmentation of pancreatic tumors and organs at risk (OARs) for MRI-guided radiotherapy. Purpose: We propose Pancreas-MedNeXt, a unified model that segments the whole pancreas, its subregions, tumors, and surrounding OARs from one MRI volume. Methods: The model uses a shared MedNeXt encoder-decoder with supervised whole-pancreas and subregion pretraining, followed by multitask fine-tuning. Three output heads predict the pancreatic subregions, tumor, and OARs (stomach, duodenum, kidneys). An annotation-aware loss allows training on partially labeled data without treating unannotated structures as background. Pancreas and tumor were evaluated by three-fold cross-validation on 142 PANTHER patients (92 diagnostic, 50 MR-Linac), subregions and OARs on Cyst-X and AMOS test sets, and pancreas and OARs on 33 external T1-weighted TotalSegmentator scans. Comparisons with nnU-Net, MedNeXt, VISTA3D, and Swin UNETR used the Dice similarity coefficient (DSC) with paired bootstrap 95% CIs. Results: Pancreas-MedNeXt achieved the highest mean pancreas and tumor DSC on diagnostic and MR-Linac MRI. Tumor DSC was 58.41% and 42.55%, exceeding task-specific MedNeXt by 4.94 (95% CI: 0.28-9.45) and 5.89 (95% CI: 2.37-9.72) percentage points, respectively. Whole-pancreas DSC was 85.73% and 70.66%. Pretraining improved head, body, and tail DSC by 2.01, 3.00, and 3.78 percentage points over training from scratch. It also achieved the highest DSC for the pancreas, duodenum, and right kidney on AMOS, and for all five organs on external MRI, exceeding the next-best method by 4.92-8.85 percentagethis http URL: A single Pancreas-MedNeXt model segments tumors, pancreatic subregions, and OARs on diagnostic and MR-Linac MRI, which could streamline contouring for pancreatic cancer radiotherapy.
  </details>

- **[Dreams Are Cheaper Than Flights: Curated World-Model Data Improves Aerial Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41030)**  `arXiv:2610.41030`  
  _Rugved Katole, Vedant Patil, Jenna Kline, Christopher Stewart_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Robot perception is increasingly data driven, yet field data is slow, costly, and sometimes disruptive to collect. Video world models offer a cheaper source: seeded with real frames, they extend a recorded scene into new footage with the deployment's sensor, altitude, and environment. We ask whether such synthetic data matches the utility of real field data and whether detectors trained with it perform in the field. Seeding a 7B-parameter world model with wildlife drone video, we more than double the training set with curated frames. On held-out videos from the same drones, mAP@50--95 rises 4.3\% when these frames are added to real data and 10.3\% when they replace it, and up to 33.5\% on an urban benchmark. Although an expert rejects an estimated 87.5\% of generated clips, the data costs about \$1,100 of GPU time, against more than \$3,200 of field labor alone for a less useful real training set. Synthetic data thus shifts data costs from fieldwork to compute.
  </details>

- **[RADI3N: GeometRic ImAge EDiting via ParametrIc 3D CoNtrol](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40973)**  `arXiv:2610.40973`  
  _Abdul Wasi, Akhil Gorugantu, Mahesh Bhosale, Anjan Dutta, Junsong Yuan, Sauradip Nag, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent diffusion-based methods have substantially advanced image editing, yet parametric 3D control at the part level remains challenging. Existing approaches either operate on whole objects with manual rigging, or revert to 2D handles at the part level. They also struggle to model appearance and texture under large transformations, leading to unrealistic outputs in geometry-intensive edits. To address these limitations, we introduce RADI3N, a framework for semantically-grounded part-aware object manipulation with parametric 3D control. Our CS-ARAP deformation module suppresses rotation-field discontinuities at part boundaries via neighborhood covariance smoothing, producing structurally consistent deformations under complex part-level edits. We further introduce an inversion-free selective texture transfer mechanism that suppresses high-frequency positional biases and scales up low-frequency semantic cues within cross-image attention. This eliminates strict spatial bias and supports semantic matching, allowing source appearance to follow the edited part onto its new geometry. Experiments demonstrate that RADI3N achieves state-of-the-art performance in geometric fidelity, viewpoint consistency, and controllability.
  </details>

- **[AVID: A Weakly Aligned RGB-Infrared Dataset and Perturbation Benchmark for Tiny Aerial Targets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40949)**  `arXiv:2610.40949`  
  _Dharini Raghavan, Vishnu Rao, Sethu Selvi S_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Long-range detection of small drones is complicated by their visual similarity to birds and by the limited detail available from ground-based cameras. Combining visible and infrared observations offers complementary information, but their usefulness depends on target size, cross-modal alignment, and image quality. We present AVID, a benchmark for studying these factors using real RGB-infrared bird-drone surveillance footage originating from the Drone Detection Dataset and curated for the IEEE ICIP 2025 Video and Image Processing Cup. AVID comprises 32,040 corresponding frame pairs from 115 videos, with 35,952 existing bird and drone annotations at $320\times256$ resolution. Targets occupy an unusually narrow size range: 68.1\% have a geometric-mean bounding-box side of 8--16 pixels, and 99.0\% meet the COCO small-object criterion. To characterize the geometric difficulty of combining the modalities, we introduce an object-relative alignment ratio and an alignment burden curve. In a sample of 300 frame pairs, 32.3\% have estimated global translations greater than 5 pixels; among the 264 pairs with matching annotations, 16.3\% exceed one median target side. We further introduce AVID-C, which simulates seven families of image degradation at five severity levels independently in RGB, infrared, or both. A fixed-feature analysis examines how scale, degradation, and added cross-modal shifts affect fitted bird-drone class overlap. At 12--16 pixels, the Bhattacharyya error bounds are 0.148 for RGB, 0.198 for infrared, and 0.046 for their combined features. These values describe separation in the specified feature and density models, not detector accuracy. Together, AVID and AVID-C support scale-aware, alignment-aware, and sensor-specific studies of tiny aerial targets.
  </details>

- **[UFMF: Radar-Guided Kinematic Priors for Real-Time 4D Occupancy Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40947)**  `arXiv:2610.40947`  
  _Zeping Zhang, Zhuoya Zhao, Robert Laganière_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Real-time 4D occupancy forecasting requires reliably extrapolating both ego motion and surrounding actor motion under strict latency budgets. Recent occupancy world models improve long-horizon fidelity through generative decoders and 3D volumetric modeling, yet their computational cost makes real-time deployment challenging. Light-weight BEV forecasters meet latency requirements but often blur or suppress fast-moving actors at longer horizons. We present UFMF (Ultra-Fast Multi-modal Forecaster), a real-time forecaster that keeps an attention-free diagonal state-space temporal core while injecting targeted kinematic and geometric structure where lightweight models typically fail. A radar-guided velocity-aware dynamic prior (VADP) decomposes future displacement into a linear extrapolation plus a learned horizon-dependent residual. The resulting per-cell flow fields transport a compact dynamic latent subspace during rollout. Two auxiliary training constraints complement this branch: world-frame dynamic supervision enforces inter-horizon trajectory coherence, and class-conditioned vertical-profile supervision regularizes BEV-to-3D lifting. UFMF operates at 122 FPS on a single RTX 4090 and reaches 0.45 fast-displacement mIoU on nuScenes, outperforming recent occupancy world models at a fraction of their computational cost.
  </details>

- **[Let LiDAR Decide Where to Look: Geometry-Measured Token Selection for Efficient Sparse Latent World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40935)**  `arXiv:2610.40935`  
  _Zeping Zhang, Zhuoya Zhao, Robert Laganière_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-centric autonomous driving models encode thousands of image tokens per frame, yet most depict sky, buildings, or empty road---content irrelevant to planning. Worse, these tokens dilute the planner's attention: we show that randomly discarding 75% of tokens already outperforms the dense model, because the planner can concentrate on the remaining driving-critical content. Building on this finding, we present Geometry-Aware Token Selection (GATS), which uses LiDAR and radar point projections to select a small fraction of image tokens before the vision backbone runs. Because the selector operates on measured 3D geometry rather than visual features, the backbone encodes only the selected patches, cutting end-to-end FLOPs by up to $18\times$. Each kept token receives a 3D anchor from its LiDAR point and is fed to a self-supervised latent world model that predicts future features and 3D displacements without box annotations. At 25% budget, GATS achieves a 3-sec L2 displacement error of 0.520 m on nuScenes and an EPDMS of 88.9 on NAVSIM---surpassing the dense baseline (0.573 m / 85.9) at $6.6\times$ fewer total FLOPs. At an extreme 10% budget (24 GFLOPs, $18\times$ reduction), it still beats the dense baseline on both nuScenes (0.537 m vs. 0.573 m L2) and NAVSIM (87.9 vs. 85.9 EPDMS). On 739 intent-critical scenarios, GATS covers 95.8% of hazardous vehicles and achieves a 0.09% collision rate---73.5% lower than the dense model (0.34%).
  </details>

- **[Sparse Autoencoders for Expert-Assisted Interpretation of Histopathology Foundation Model Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40905)**  `arXiv:2610.40905`  
  _Šimon Šmída, Wanda Benesova_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Histopathology foundation models learn rich embedding representations, but their internal structure remains difficult to inspect. Concept-level interpretability is particularly relevant in computational pathology because pathologists reason through recurring morphological and tissue-level patterns, including tissue architecture, cellular morphology, staining variation, and artifacts. In this work, we investigate whether sparse autoencoders (SAEs) can decompose embeddings from histopathology foundation models into sparse latent features suitable for expert-assisted interpretation. We train vanilla and Batch-TopK SAE variants on NCT-CRC embeddings, using CONCH as the primary foundation model and UNI2-h as an additional robustness evaluation, and characterize the resulting latent features through reconstruction fidelity, sparsity, coherence, label enrichment, and downstream class-signal preservation. The learned sparse representations preserve most downstream tissue-class information, and their latent features retrieve more coherent and label-concentrated patch groups than raw embedding dimensions or PCA components. Discussions with pathology experts further motivated a WSI-level latent visualization workflow that places feature activations into larger tissue context. Overall, the additional UNI2-h evaluation suggests that the observed behavior is not specific to CONCH, supporting the use of sparse autoencoders for expert-assisted analysis of histopathology foundation-model embeddings, while emphasizing that latent features should be treated as candidate concepts requiring pathological assessment rather than automatically validated histopathological concepts.
  </details>

- **[AquaProbe: Efficient Adaptation of Frozen Vision Models for Underwater Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40900)**  `arXiv:2610.40900`  
  _Md Shihabul Islam Shovo, Nishi Kanta Paul, Trung Dong, Xiaomin Lin_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reliable visual perception is essential for safe autonomous underwater exploration. Yet vision models pretrained on terrestrial images become less reliable underwater, where turbidity, backscatter, and wavelength-dependent attenuation alter scene appearance. Existing adaptation methods either spread adapters equally across the encoder depth or select layers using the divergence between two fixed domains. In this paper, we introduce AquaProbe for measuring how the location of the domain gap changes with water type and imaging range. AquaProbe separates newly introduced divergence from that accumulated through a frozen vision encoder using paired clean and physically rendered images and allocates a fixed LoRA budget according to the measured profile. The segmentation mean Intersection-over-Union (mIoU) for the proportional LoRA rank allocation, based on the measured profile, is 70.1; this is a better result than uniform LoRA rank allocation by 1.9 mIoU points while having the same parameter budget of 0.59 million adapter parameters. The three-way router, conditioned on estimated condition clusters, achieves 70.6 mIoU on the standard SUIM split and 63.4 AUDRC under held-out degradation.
  </details>

- **[Spatial Atlas-Informed Loss Ensembles for Whole-Body PSMA PET/CT Metastasis Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40834)**  `arXiv:2610.40834`  
  _Grant N. Chau, Bashirul A. Biswas, Biratal Wagle, Matthew E. Maeder, Michael S. Leapman, James B. Yu, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing whole-body PSMA PET/CT prostate metastasis segmentation methods generally rely on images alone, without explicitly incorporating where metastases tend to occur. We investigate whether a population metastasis atlas can improve lesion detection when integrated into model training. We constructed voxel-wise mean and standard deviation maps from 375 expert-annotated training scans and used them to weight the cross-entropy loss of an nnU-Net. The additional penalties for missed lesions and false positives depend on spatial atlas statistics and diminish as predictions become confidently correct. Seven configurations were compared on validation data; three were combined by majority voting into SAILS (Spatial Atlas-Informed Loss enSemble). We compared SAILS with a baseline nnU-Net and inference-time atlas fusion on AutoPET~III (110 disease-positive cases) and Deep-PSMA (100 external cases). No atlas or registration is needed at inference. Results: On AutoPET~III, SAILS improved whole-body Dice (0.531 vs. 0.520; p=0.006), reduced missed-lesion volume (9.5 vs. 12.1 cc/case; p<0.01), and increased pooled lesion sensitivity (0.857 vs. 0.843; p=0.004). On Deep-PSMA, SAILS detected 126 additional reference lesions (sensitivity 0.769 vs. 0.751; p<0.001), without a significant whole-body Dice change (-0.009; p=0.36). High-risk bone Dice improved in both cohorts (0.576 vs. 0.552 and 0.667 vs. 0.653; both p<0.01). False-positive volume increased by 2.3 and 18.2 cc/case, respectively (p<0.001). Atlas-weighted training improves lesion detection, especially in high-risk bone, without test-time atlas registration, but increases false positives.
  </details>

- **[EgoHSI3R: Online 4D Hand-Scene Interaction Reconstruction from Egocentric Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40832)**  `arXiv:2610.40832`  
  _Mingzhi Pei, Zhangchen Ye, Ruoqu Chen, Liu Cao, Mengdi Xu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Egocentric videos offer an abundant source of hand-scene interactions for embodied learning, which requires recovering hands, camera, and scene consistently in a shared 3D world. Existing methods focus on either camera-frame hands or world-space hand trajectories, without jointly recovering the scene pointmaps or interaction information. Building a complete reconstruction from such specialized predictions therefore typically requires per-sequence optimization. We propose EgoHSI3R, a unified feed-forward framework for online 4D hand-scene interaction reconstruction from egocentric monocular video. To jointly recover these components, it maintains a recurrent scene representation that accumulates geometric evidence over time and provides a shared world space and scale. We introduce hand-centric prompts that fuse pretrained hand priors and local scene context, while MANO vertex queries attend to scene features to predict dense per-vertex contact. These designs enable a single network to jointly estimate camera motion, dense scene geometry, world-space bilateral MANO hands, and per-vertex hand contact. Despite operating causally, EgoHSI3R achieves the lowest world-space hand joint error on ARCTIC among the evaluated methods, while offering faster inference than existing world-space hand reconstruction pipelines and competitive camera and scene reconstruction accuracy.
  </details>

- **[Drone-PUR: From Visual Clue Discovery to Understanding and Reasoning in UAV Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40801)**  `arXiv:2610.40801`  
  _Jiahe Wu, Bing Cao, Xingyue Guo, Ruipu Zhao, Beiming Yu, Jiekang Feng, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The reasoning capabilities of vision-language models (VLMs) can be limited by incomplete or inaccurate visual observations in the general domain. In unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) scenes, small targets, subtle attributes, and changing viewpoints further complicate perception and understanding. Therefore, we introduce Drone-PUR, a dataset and benchmark organized around visual clue discovery, interpretation, and integration, corresponding to the three capability dimensions of perception, understanding, and reasoning. Reasoning samples are designed to require models to combine multiple visual clues to derive visually grounded conclusions. Its 11 tasks provide 175k training and 20k test samples spanning single images, image sequences, cross-modal images, and multiple viewpoints. All test samples undergo human review. Evaluating 22 general-purpose VLMs reveals persistent limitations in spatial judgment and cross-modal and cross-view reasoning. Across models, stronger perception is associated with better reasoning, while single-task fine-tuning further reveals selective bidirectional transfer between these capabilities. To examine domain adaptation, we develop UAV-PUR from Qwen3-VL through staged fine-tuning with auxiliary supervision. Its 4B and 8B variants improve overall multiple-choice accuracy by 18.33 and 17.52 percentage points over their pretrained counterparts, respectively. The dataset and project resources are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[DyCraft: An Agentic System for Long-Horizon 4D Scene Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40754)**  `arXiv:2610.40754`  
  _Jeff Tan, Hang Dai, Zeqian Long, Yanzhe Lyu, R. Kenny Jones, Shangzhe Wu, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  While frontier coding agents can generate high-quality 3D scenes and articulated assets, they struggle to create coherent long-horizon animations. When equipped with general-purpose harnesses, they frequently produce physically implausible outputs, generating objects that interpenetrate, lack physical support, or move unnaturally. Such bare agents struggle to repair these errors because they default to scripting the full animation in one pass, making it difficult to verify their work and disentangle complex spatiotemporal constraints. Drawing on the practice of human animators, we introduce DyCraft, a system for animated 3D scene generation that breaks animation into steps that coding agents can more reliably solve. DyCraft first expands the user's prompt into a detailed plan and builds a static scene. It then creates a 3D storyboard of key animation poses, where each frame is a static 3D scene that the agent can build and debug in isolation. Storyboarding decomposes the problem into pairs of verified start and end states, allowing the agent to author local motion between them with a collection of motion authoring tools. We evaluate DyCraft on 24 long-horizon, multi-shot animation tasks covering humanoid robots, human and animal characters, and physical mechanisms. Compared with a native GPT-6 agent in Codex and prior agentic baselines, DyCraft substantially reduces physical violations and generates higher-quality animated 3D scenes that are more often preferred by human raters.
  </details>

- **[PathoJev: Efficient Pathology Decisions from Shared Visual Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40752)**  `arXiv:2610.40752`  
  _Chengyang Zhang, Wenchuan Zhang, Bo Li, Xinyu Liu, Jiaming Yang, Mengran Li, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pathology workflows require multiple visual judgments about the same slide, from identifying tumor type to assessing grade and invasion. Generating and parsing a separate textual response for each structured question adds latency between visual evidence and usable decisions. Building on the System One paradigm, we introduce PathoJev, a pathology model that directly predicts answer probabilities for whole-slide and region-level assessment. PathoJev encodes each slide once and evaluates independent question suffixes in batches over the shared visual context. Reusing the hybrid backbone's recurrent states further limits the computation that grows with slide-context length for each additional question. Across three whole-slide benchmarks, the 9B and 27B models achieve mean accuracies of 77.2% and 78.1%, exceeding SlideChat by 5.4 and 6.3 percentage points. Both scales also maintain competitive accuracy on five ROI benchmarks. On WSI-Bench, the 9B model runs 9.1x faster than SlideChat. Shared context accelerates eight-question workloads by 6.9x and 5.9x over separate prefix encoding, rising to 11.2x and 8.9x for 64 questions. These results provide a reusable probabilistic interface for combining multiple visual judgments in whole-slide and local tissue assessment. Code:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[BiHashFormer: Hash-Driven Dual-Branch Transformer for Efficient Object Detection in HRW Shots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39937)**  `arXiv:2610.39937`  
  _Jingchen Huang, Wenxi Li, Chenyang Lyu, Moran Liu, Haozhe Lin, Yuchen Guo_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent advances in gigapixel-level imaging have brought High-Resolution Wide shots to the forefront of research. However, these images present significant challenges: extreme sparsity of foreground, gigapixel-level resolutions and interleaving of foreground and background. This causes traditional detectors and attention mechanisms to be hindered by background, resulting in inefficiency and inaccuracy. To tackle this problem, we propose BiHashFormer, a dual sparsified branch transformer built on hashing. RoI Selector will predict the foreground proportion for top-k selecting target-containing windows. The compression branch processes windows selected and refines attention into HashAttention by discarding half of the key-value pairs, reducing cost and improving efficiency. The compensation branch uses HashMiner to perform low-cost hash searches in the remaining regions, recovering features of missed objects. Experiments confirm that the selection relationship in HashAttention is one-way and reveal the preference of different queries for keys. In experiments on the gigapixel benchmark PANDA, BiHashFormer reduces 37.5% backbone FLOPs while improving AP50 to 81.0%. Moreover, this parallel dual-branch structure allows the model to avoid inference delays caused by inter-branch dependencies, while achieving much greater stability than single-branch models, specially with low rates of RoI selection.
  </details>

- **[GROME: GROunding Factorized MEmory into Video World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39812)**  `arXiv:2610.39812`  
  _Fang Yu, Xiaoxue Wu, Haiyu Zhang, Yangfu Li, Botong Zhao, Yu Qiao, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Long-horizon consistency is essential for video world models, yet it becomes difficult to maintain once observed content falls outside the context window. Existing memory mechanisms either retrieve history as implicit context, which remains constrained by context capacity and entangles static and dynamic information or consolidate history into explicit global representations, which struggle to model evolving dynamics and accumulate geometric errors. To overcome these limitations, we introduce GROME, a novel factorized memory framework that augments video world models for long-horizon coherence across both static scenes and dynamic entities. GROME operates via three synergistic components: 1) Static-Dynamic Factorization decouples historical evidence into static and dynamic components, preserving static scene geometry and dynamic entity states separately; 2) Local Memory Retrieval selects spatially aligned static history to eliminate geometric drift alongside the latest dynamic entity states to ensure temporal continuity; 3) Training-Free Memory Injection seamlessly grounds the factorized memory into video world models via their native conditioning pathways. Experiments on long-horizon revisit scenarios demonstrate that GROME substantially enhances scene persistence and dynamic entity fidelity over existing methods, all while preserving the visual quality and controllability of the frozen backbone without additional training. Beyond self-rollouts, GROME readily accommodates external observations as plug-and-play memory, unlocking flexible and controllable video continuation and scene exploration.
  </details>

- **[Seeing and Imagining as One: Unified Video Generation and 4D Perception for Embodied World Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39232)**  `arXiv:2610.39232`  
  _Haitang Feng, Zhuoman Liu, Zhennan Wang, Sheng Wang, Juelin Li, Chengzhi Zhao, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Embodied world models should not only imagine plausible futures, but also perceive the geometric, semantic, and motion structure of the evolving world. We present GenSight, a unified embodied world model that brings future video generation and dense perception into a single pretrained video diffusion transformer. GenSight uses a unified spatiotemporal representation for video, depth, embodiment segmentation, and first-frame-relative 2D flow. Built on a shared DiT backbone, GenSight uses lightweight task modulation to predict all modalities within the same architecture. All tasks are jointly optimized within a unified velocity prediction space. At inference, GenSight supports two complementary modes: multi-step future video generation with aligned dense predictions and single-step dense perception from an observed video. By unifying modality representation, model architecture, and prediction space, GenSight avoids separate modality-specific pipelines and couples appearance, geometry, semantics, and motion in a shared representation. It promotes cross-modal consistency and allows dense perception to benefit from the spatiotemporal priors learned through video generation. Trained on embodied manipulation data spanning multiple robot embodiments and human-hand interactions, GenSight demonstrates strong performance in both video generation and dense 4D perception, highlighting the effectiveness of unifying seeing and imagining for embodied world modeling.
  </details>

- **[VEvo-Harness: Causally-Guarded Self-Evolving Harness for Agentic Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39158)**  `arXiv:2610.39158`  
  _Yuan Wang, Yuhang Yu, Mengting Chen, Tianrun Xu, Zihao Pan, Xuetao Feng, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The central bottleneck in visual generation and editing has migrated from achieving pixel-level fidelity to satisfying densely constrained user specifications through increasingly agentic workflows. Prevailing agentic generation pipelines remain shallow generate-verify-rewrite loops treating VLM judgments as coarse visual feedback, either discarding repair experience or admitting ungated updates that poison skill library. With memorize and govern periods left open, they degrade into ungoverned iterators of repeated trial-and-error rather than continual self-evolving harness. Towards this issue, we present \textbf{VEvo-Harness}, the first causally-guarded continual self-evolving harness system for agentic visual generation. VEvo-Harness introduces four coupled innovations: (1) \textit{a visual traceback} that densifies sparse judgments into root-cause attributions with a hierarchical verification tree; (2) \textit{a visual continual governance} that admits only causally valid skill patches through counterfactual dual-arm image generation replay; (3) \textit{a lifecycle-managed harness state} that hierarchically distills root-cause-anchored episodes across working, experience, and pattern tiers into a persistent skill library of routable and causally verified capabilities; (4) \textit{a heterogeneous sandbox policy} that orchestrates a diverse action space of repair primitives, dynamically routing cost-aware interventions based on root causes to select the most effective strategy. VEvo-Harness surpasses state-of-the-art agentic methods and rivals proprietary generators (\textit{e.g.}, GPT-Image, Nano-Pro) while maintaining the continual harness evolution and generalization.
  </details>

- **[Knowledge-structured and evidence-aware semantic self-distillation for glomerular pathology foundation model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38346)**  `arXiv:2610.38346`  
  _Qiming He, Jing Li, Zihan Wang, Tian Guan, Yifei Ma, Zimo Zhao, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual foundation models learn rich pathological morphology but lack explicit concept-level supervision. Vision--language pretraining can provide this supervision but requires costly image--text pairs. Here, we introduce GloSAGE, a semantic self-distillation framework that uses lesion profiles predicted by a pretrained visual teacher to construct structured pathology descriptions. Reliability-guided selection identifies image--text anchors, while Knowledge-Structured Pathology Text Synthesis and Evidence-Guided Negative-pair Construction account for partially observed lesions. Image--text alignment trains projection layers while retaining the frozen visual encoder; an intermediate Semantic Transfer Layer supplies features for downstream tasks. Developed on GloMillion and evaluated across seven glomerulus- and case-level cohorts, GloSAGE supported zero-shot lesion recognition, image--text retrieval, hard-negative discrimination and concept-intervention analyses. It outperformed its source visual model in most lesion-recognition tasks and improved lesion grading, while supporting competitive case-level disease classification, injury grading and baseline renal function estimation. Ablations supported the combined contributions of knowledge-structured text and evidence-guided negatives, and the intermediate transfer layer improved mean lesion-recognition ROC-AUC relative to the complete projection output. A frozen language model conditioned on GloSAGE features generated glomerular descriptions. These findings suggest that teacher-derived lesion profiles can provide annotation-efficient semantic supervision for extending a glomerular visual foundation model.
  </details>

- **[Hidden Commitment: When Language Models Silently Pick a Side and How Steering Can Surface It](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38296)**  `arXiv:2610.38296`  
  _Samuel Dawit Assefa, Jae Won Cho_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Language-model answers are often treated as a visible record of the internal decision that produced them. We test this assumption in conflicting-context question answering, where two supplied documents support incompatible answers. A model can process the disagreement and still return a one-sided answer, even under prompts that ask it to disclose conflict. In Qwen 0.8B, hidden source commitment is captured by a residual source-choice direction whose sign tracks which document the answer uses. Sparse middle-layer MLP writers feed the same coordinate, and steering the coordinate changes the chosen source. A transfer run on Gemma 3 1B preserves the source-choice readout and sparse-write bridge under LLM-audited labels. Conflict disclosure follows a different pattern, appearing weaker, later-layer, and only partially steerable. These results support a practical monitoring strategy for retrieval systems that detects silent source commitment with residual readouts and pairs conflict-surfacing prompts with disclosure steering when the model has selected a side under disagreement.
  </details>

- **[Sexual dimorphism and sex-specific features of pediatric hand bone shapes via statistical shape modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35143)**  `arXiv:2610.35143`  
  _Edwin Tay, Amir A. Zadpoor, Nazli Tümer_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Understanding the envelope of shape variation encompassing both sexual dimorphism and growth-related changes in living tissue is a non-trivial task. In this work, we use statistical shape models (SSMs) and analyze demographic-stratified shape parameters to quantify sexual dimorphism and sex-specific shape changes. To this end, we developed a two-stage detection-segmentation framework that accurately segmented $n = 27664$ unique bone masks from a radiographic dataset of $n = 1456$ subjects with diverse ages and sexes (HD95 = $1.512 \pm 0.005$, ASSD $0.449 \pm 001$, Dice = $0.987 \pm 0.00004$, IoU = $0.975 \pm 0.0001$). Following SSM fitting, our follow-up analyses of stratified shape parameters highlighted the morphology of sexual dimorphisms for each bone and demonstrated sex-specific differences in shape development. Our results were consistent with existing literature and provided an unambiguous, data-driven methodology for characterizing tissue shape and its population-scale variation. This approach provides a potential methodological foundation for developing population-based models of anatomical variation and could be extended to investigate tissue growth and shape changes over time. Future work should evaluate the applicability of this methodology on alternative anatomical structures and imaging modalities, with the goal of validating these growth trajectories on longitudinal data.
  </details>

- **[An Agentic AI Workflow for Liver Lesion Segmentation and Structured Radiology Reporting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34599)**  `arXiv:2610.34599`  
  _Fatma Ahmed Mansour, Rahatara Ferdousi, Manar Fathy Elshenawy_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Despite advances in automated liver lesion segmentation, a gap remains between image-derived predictions and clinically interpretable radiology reports. Bridging this gap requires quantitative measurements that are both reproducible and traceable. We present an evidence-grounded agentic AI framework that connects liver and lesion segmentation with deterministic quantification and structured reporting. TotalSegmentator-based nnU-Net models delineate the liver, Couinaud segments, and lesions, while computational tools extract volumes, dimensions, counts, and anatomical locations. A large language model (LLM) organizes these measurements into a predefined reporting schema, with numerical consistency checks against source measurements. On 51 LiTS cases, the segmentation stage achieved a liver Dice score of 0.947, lesion sensitivity of 75.6%, and positive predictive value of 68.9%. Exploratory screening of 25,494 MERLIN reports identified 10,208 (40.0%) with liver-related abnormalities. By connecting quantitative imaging evidence with agentic report generation, this work takes a step toward human-centered medical AI that prioritizes traceability and clinical relevance.
  </details>

- **[Reading, Writing, Sharing, and Interacting with the World through Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31668)**  `arXiv:2610.31668`  
  _Shengqiong Wu, Meng Luo, Bobo Li, Ziwei Liu, Shuicheng Yan, Ming-Hsuan Yang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video foundation models have advanced rapidly in understanding and generating visual sequences, while video world models seek to capture the underlying dynamics of the world they depict. Despite their different objectives, both concern the relationship between video observations and an evolving world. This survey adopts world modeling as a unifying lens for examining these developments. Viewing an observed video as a partial, lossy, and temporally ordered projection of a latent temporal world, we organize the literature around four complementary operations: reading infers world properties from observed video; writing synthesizes video from specified or learned world conditions; sharing integrates understanding and generation through common modeling mechanisms; and interacting predicts and updates world states under actions and evolving observations. This perspective distinguishes temporal coherence in observed or generated sequences from consistency in the underlying world state. Across tasks, representations, models, datasets, and benchmarks, we trace a shift in emphasis from clip-level recognition and perceptual fidelity toward persistent state, controllable evolution, physical and causal validity, and evaluation grounded in interaction and downstream decisions, and we distill the open challenges that follow.
  </details>

- **[Open-Vocabulary Models and Label Propagation for Uncertainty-aware Pseudo-Labeling of Object Detection Datasets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29788)**  `arXiv:2610.29788`  
  _Jonathan Klees, Timo Heller, Matthias Rottmann_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Object detection models rely on large-scale annotated datasets that require substantial manual annotation effort. The labeling costs further increase when annotation uncertainty is captured through soft labels, as multiple annotations must be collected for each object. We address this challenge by introducing a semi-supervised framework for the uncertainty-aware annotation of object detection datasets. Our approach leverages open-vocabulary object detectors to generate region proposals and semantically meaningful object representations. Based on these representations, we construct an object-level similarity graph and propagate probabilistic label information. Our framework assigns soft labels to previously unlabeled objects, quantifying both aleatoric and epistemic uncertainty. We evaluate our method on four object detection benchmarks with soft-label annotations and demonstrate that high-quality annotations can be obtained with only a small fraction of manually labeled objects. Furthermore, we show that detectors trained on semi-automatically annotated data achieve performance comparable to models trained on fully manually annotated data. We achieve state-of-the-art downstream detection performance for the task of semi-supervised object detection on the COCO dataset, with 40.5% AP virtually matching the performance of the model trained on fully manually constructed data (40.9%) at only 1% of labeling costs. These results highlight the potential of open-vocabulary models and graph-based label propagation for efficient, accurate and uncertainty-aware pseudo-labeling.
  </details>

- **[Learning Goal-Conditioned Subgoals through Frozen Hierarchical World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26179)**  `arXiv:2610.26179`  
  _Xiang Kaibo, Liu Qingfeng_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Hierarchical world models plan over long horizons by passing predicted states from a coarse model to a lower-level controller. Choosing these states can be expensive: the upper-level planner repeatedly differentiates through candidate macro-action trajectories. We replace this search with a lightweight goal-conditioned network trained through the frozen high-level predictor. The network supplies a conditioning vector, while the predictor generates the next latent waypoint. Training uses hindsight goals and state-prediction losses on offline trajectories, without search-solution targets or macro-action labels. Inference retains an autoregressive high-level rollout, but requires no high-level candidate optimization. We evaluate this approach on the shared-latent HWM checkpoints distributed with H-JEPA, using three backbone seeds and three head initializations per backbone. With a common evaluation protocol, mean success is 70.4% on Cube, 48.7% on Push-T, 100.0% on FourRoom, and 71.1% on Visual AntMaze. The method improves over the corresponding HWM search baseline on three environments and has the same mean on Push-T. Dedicated measurements on an NVIDIA L40S give 1.28-4.39x end-to-end speedups for batched evaluation. Ablations identify state-adaptive conditioning as a consistent contributor, while predictor-mediated subgoals and unrestricted conditions have environment-dependent benefits.
  </details>

- **[PACE: Pareto-Adaptive Compression for Efficient Native MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24005)**  `arXiv:2610.24005`  
  _Lei Tian, Chunzheng Zhu, Kai Wu, Yifan Zhang, Haihua Yang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Native multimodal large language models (native MLLMs) jointly encode visual and linguistic sequences within a unified autoregressive Transformer, emerging as a promising paradigm for multimodal understanding. Unlike conventional ViT-based MLLMs relying on external vision encoders, ViT-free native MLLMs tokenize pixel patches directly, potentially suffering severe visual token redundancy. A pilot study confirms this: unlike ViT-based models that degrade under compression, ViT-free MLLMs tolerate aggressive compression with near-lossless or even improved performance. Yet existing compression methods target ViT-based settings, leaving native MLLMs, which stand to gain the most, unexplored. To close this gap, we introduce an analysis pipeline centered on token discriminability and cross-dimensional heterogeneous redundancy, revealing that (i) redundancy is distributed independently and heterogeneously along height and width axes, and (ii) early-layer visual tokens are indiscriminable and uniformly redundant. Guided by these findings, we propose PACE (Pareto-Adaptive Compression for Efficient Native MLLMs), the first visual token compression framework tailored for native MLLMs with Multimodal-RoPE. Leveraging dimensional decoupling of positional encoding, PACE decomposes attention into two orthogonal spatial saliency estimates along height and width, gated by a positional attention signal, yielding direction-aware importance with negligible overhead. It further casts token budget allocation as Pareto optimization over performance preservation, inference efficiency, and spatial coverage, jointly identifying optimal compression depths and retention ratios. Experiments show PACE maintains near-lossless performance at only 25%-50% visual token retention, even improving several benchmarks, while delivering over 2x FLOPs reduction.
  </details>

- **[PixelMoE: Scaling Pixel-Space Diffusion with LLM-Style Mixture-of-Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18857)**  `arXiv:2610.18857`  
  _Ruixiao Dong, Jianmin Bao, Bozhi Luan, Zijie Meng, Xin Tao, Pengfei Wan, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pixel-space diffusion models provide an appealing alternative to latent-space diffusion models by directly modeling raw images without relying on a learned tokenizer. However, scaling pixel-space models has predominantly followed dense strategies, tightly coupling model capacity with active computation. In this work, we show that standard LLM-style Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures can be effectively transferred to pixel-space diffusion. Building on this finding, we introduce PixelMoE, a pixel-space generative framework that sparsely scales model capacity with standard LLM-style MoE, without diffusion-specific routing mechanisms or auxiliary routing objectives. Specifically, PixelMoE combines standard Token-Choice (TC) routing with fine-grained routed experts for input-adaptive sparse computation, a shared expert for capturing common representations, and a bias-based load-balancing strategy. To complement sparse capacity scaling, PixelMoE further introduces Pixel-Space Hierarchical Flow Matching, which varies spatial resolution along the denoising trajectory from global structure formation to fine-grained detail refinement. At comparable active parameter counts, PixelMoE consistently outperforms its dense counterparts across model scales, demonstrating effective sparse capacity scaling in pixel space. On class-conditional ImageNet-1K, PixelMoE achieves FID scores of 1.47 at 256x256 and 1.53 at 512x512 without representation alignment or perceptual losses, comparing favorably with leading pixel-space and latent-space generative models. Code and pretrained models are available atthis https URLandthis https URL, respectively.
  </details>

- **[Why VLMs Miss Small Objects, and When Zooming In Is Safe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09313)**  `arXiv:2610.09313`  
  _Junzhe Shi, Yuan Gan, Shida Jiang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Screenshots, documents, engineering drawings, shelves and maps hold many small objects of kinds that change from task to task, and vision-language models (VLMs) promise to find them from an instruction and an example. Yet they see each image through a fixed token budget and miss small objects, so every user, and every agent that calls a model, must choose whether to send an image whole, raise its resolution, split it, or zoom. We show that VLMs miss small objects for two reasons a caller can estimate before any call: an object gets too few visual tokens (S), and a call must search and report too much (L). Raising S by a factor a multiplies the tokens needed to see an image by a^2, while splitting at the same scale lowers L at a cost at most linear in the number of views. Under two monotonicity assumptions, splitting cannot lower expected recall if no view zooms out and views overlap by one object, and neither part can be dropped; every such split pays a minimum token cost, and a simple rule pays it up to an explicit factor. Better models need less S, but an image can be arbitrarily large while a call's budget is fixed, so splitting does not become obsolete, for zoom agents either. In about 187,000 requests to 12 VLMs, up to GPT-6.1, Claude Opus 5.5 and Gemini 4, on controlled images 24 of 40 predicted orderings hold significantly and none is violated; splits that never zoom out raised recall on average over models wherever S was below 1.5, and splits that zoom out lowered it in 19 of 21 model and data settings. The rule raised recall significantly in five of ten settings, by up to 0.28 (from 0.62 to 0.90), and precision and F1 in six, at a token cost known before any call.
  </details>

- **[RDGSplat: Render-Dedicated Geometry for Novel View Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09173)**  `arXiv:2610.09173`  
  _Zhijie Zheng, Xinhao Xiang, Jiawei Zhang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent 3D foundation models extend to novel view synthesis by carrying a Gaussian head on the representation they already use for reconstruction. However, their rendering quality lags far behind their reconstruction accuracy, because the renderer reads a geometry and a representation that are both optimized for metric reconstruction. Prior methods alleviate this by updating the backbone weights, but they thereby sacrifice the metric predictions the model was built for. To this end, we propose RDGSplat, which decodes a geometry dedicated to rendering from a frozen 3D foundation model while keeping its metric predictions exact. In particular, we devise Render-Dedicated Geometry Decoding, which duplicates the pretrained decoders and optimizes the duplicates under photometric supervision alone. Then, a Target-Pose Conditioned Adapter is introduced to adapt the representation those decoders read towards rendering, conditioned on the predicted target pose. Extensive experiments show that RDGSplat improves novel view synthesis for three feed-forward models of two architectures on four benchmarks, with every pretrained weight frozen. On RE10K, it raises WM2.0 from 20.918 to 25.149dB by training 205.5M added parameters against a frozen 1.4B backbone, and leaves its predicted depth and pose unchanged.
  </details>

- **[Vision Transformer Ensembles for Panoramic Street Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06063)**  `arXiv:2610.06063`  
  _Yunus Serhat Bıçakçı_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Semantic segmentation of street panoramas can support detailed descriptions of urban environments, yet small datasets and unequal training costs make model selection difficult. This paper documents the two stages of a submission to the PalmCity challenge. In the first stage, nine pretrained segmentation systems are compared using approximately equal computation budgets, and the two leading candidates are trained with three random seeds. An equal probability average of three Encoder only Mask Transformer models with DINOv3 features, evaluated at three image scales with horizontal reflection, reaches 60.95% mean intersection over union on the 84 image validation split and 57.08% on the hidden test leaderboard, first in the snapshot of 5 October 2026. In the second stage, color and crop augmentation, rare class sampling, larger inputs, further architectures, and post processing are tested under the same update budget, and candidates are compared through a paired image bootstrap with selection rules fixed before the results. Training at 768 by 1536 and 896 by 1792 pixels gives the largest gains. The selected ensemble of four models scores 64.53% on validation when trained on the training images alone. Retrained on the training and validation images, it receives 61.54% mean intersection over union and 71.93% mean F1 on the hidden test leaderboard, first among five entries in the snapshot of 30 October 2026. Segment Anything voting, instance mask overlays, and ensembles with other architectures all reduce the validation score. Because the validation images guided many choices and the final models were trained on them, the validation scores are optimistic and the test score is the only independent measurement. The study reports all runs, comparisons, costs, and limits, and releases the code and models.
  </details>

- **[Gaussian Splatting-based Volumetric Video Compression with Sparse 4D Anchors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33969)**  `arXiv:2609.33969`  
  _Ge Gao, Siyue Teng, Chanqgi Wang, Fan Zhang, Nantheera Anantrasirichai, Jui Chiu Chiang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Immersive video communication requires photorealistic, compact dynamic scene representations that support efficient rendering. 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) is a promising approach, but dynamic 3DGS remains difficult to compress due to its dense primitives and spatiotemporal redundancy. Anchor-based formulations improve compactness through sparse scaffolds that share geometry and appearance across primitives. However, existing designs typically deform a single canonical scaffold and condition each primitive solely on its associated anchor, limiting their ability to model non-local dynamics and disocclusions and exploit inter-anchor correlations. To address these limitations, we propose SAGA, a volumetric video codec built on Sparse Anchor-assisted GAussian splatting representations. SAGA represents a dynamic scene using hierarchical sparse anchors in 4D space-time. Coordinate-based INR decoders generate fine anchors and Gaussian primitives from features obtained through kernel interpolation among nearby anchors, enabling parameter sharing across spatiotemporal structures. A fixed-size memory with a novel orthogonality-informed slot-update strategy further captures long-range dependencies among unstructured anchors for entropy coding. Empirically, \name achieves PSNR-based BD-rate reductions of 90.88%, 64.30% and 65.14% relative to GIFStream, TED-4DGS, and OMG4, respectively, on the Neu3D testset.
  </details>

- **[One Step Is Optimal: Unconditional Rectified Flows as Noise2Noise Denoisers for Low-Dose CT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31670)**  `arXiv:2609.31670`  
  _Timothy Sereda, Debesh Jha_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We study rectified flows for denoising without clean training targets. For an unconditional rectified flow trained on Noise2Noise pairs, the optimal velocity field yields the minimum mean squared error (MMSE) denoiser in one step. In a jointly Gaussian model, we show that integrating the flow before applying its denoising readout increases error with integration time. The flow transports between noisy distributions, so its endpoint is not a clean-image estimate. Experiments on low-dose CT and Gaussian-corrupted natural images follow this pattern: one-step flow performs similarly to direct regression on the same pairs, whereas additional Euler steps reduce PSNR. A direct regressor using spatially separated, similar patches gives our best label-free CT result, a $1.99$\,dB gain over the noisy input. We report these results in CTDenoiser, a benchmark covering five architectures, supervised and self-supervised training, and two per-image methods. Supervised CNNs gain about $4$\,dB, while label-free performance depends strongly on how training pairs account for correlated CT noise. A channelized Hotelling observer also shows that fidelity gains need not improve lesion detectability: detectability falls at $160$\,HU despite higher PSNR and SSIM, while changes at $40$--$80$\,HU are small. Code, model definitions, and data-loading utilities will be released publicly; the repository link is withheld for anonymous review.
  </details>

- **[DRT: Dense Reasoning Trace for Efficient and Grounded Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21675)**  `arXiv:2609.21675`  
  _Wan Xu, Yuanfan Guo, Kevin Han, LaLa Chen, Wangmeng Zuo_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Despite the remarkable progress in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs), prevailing Chain-of-Thought (CoT) paradigms remain confined to the natural-language expression space. Consequently, they inherently incur excessive linguistic overhead, leading to information dilution and weak visual grounding. To address this challenge, we propose Dense Reasoning Trace (DRT), a paradigm that departs from natural-language-centered CoT by expressing reasoning as compact structured traces, which include concise intermediate states with symbolic connectors and disentangle visual observations from logical deductions. First, we introduce the Dense Trace Initialization to internalize the DRT reasoning mode into the model, substantially improving token efficiency while preserving visual evidence. To further enable the model to faithfully capture the logical relations within traces, we propose the Trace-Grounded Reinforcement Learning framework, which builds reference traces through a tri-perspective verification pipeline and employs Trace-Grounded GRPO with structured rewards, encouraging the model to generate concise DRT-style traces with reduced hallucination and stronger logical grounding. Extensive experiments on challenging reasoning benchmarks show that DRT achieves 5.5$\times$ token efficiency improvement while improving 1.3 accuracy points over the Qwen3-VL baseline. These findings suggest that complex multimodal reasoning may not require verbose natural-language traces, opening a more efficient path for next-generation MLLMs. Our code and data are available at:this https URL
  </details>

- **[SV-WAM: An Efficient Surround-View World-Action Model for End-to-End Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03602)**  `arXiv:2609.03602`  
  _Jinyang Wang, Shiwei Li, Junjian Wang, Zhiqiang Deng, Jianbin Gao, Yihang Zhao, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  World models (WMs) have demonstrated strong potential for end-to-end autonomous driving by learning predictive representations of future scene dynamics. However, generating future videos during inference introduces substantial computational overhead, leading many recent driving WMs to adopt a single front camera as input for efficient deployment. This design restricts spatial coverage in safety-critical maneuvers such as lane changes, merges, and turns. To address this limitation, we propose SV-WAM, a surround-view world-action model (WAM) that preserves full six-camera observations while maintaining efficient inference. SV-WAM leverages future-video prediction as dense training supervision for action learning within a shared generative model, rather than as an inference-time output. At the core of this design is an action-centered causal mask that prevents action tokens from attending to future-video tokens during joint action-video denoising. Consequently, the video branch can be discarded at deployment, enabling efficient action-only planning. Furthermore, we introduce a differentiable drivable-area compliance regularizer that penalizes vehicle-footprint corners approaching or crossing drivable boundaries, improving planning safety and boundary awareness. Extensive experiments on the closed-loop NAVSIMv2 benchmark and the open-loop nuScenes benchmark demonstrate that SV-WAM achieves state-of-the-art planning performance with low inference latency and competitive zero-shot transfer capability.
  </details>

- **[HOPE: Hand-Object Pressure Estimation from Monocular Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06192)**  `arXiv:2608.06192`  
  _Subin Jeon, Byungjun Kim, Hanbyul Joo_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Estimating physical pressure from vision is essential for understanding contact-rich hand-object interaction. However, prior vision-based pressure estimation methods are largely limited to planar surfaces and single image input, making them difficult to apply to dynamic hand-object interaction with diverse objects. We instead formulate pressure estimation as a hand-centric video prediction problem with monocular video as input. This formulation predicts temporally evolving per-vertex normal pressure and contact directly on the hand mesh, yielding a unified output space independent of object shape and sensor layout. Building on this formulation, we propose \textbf{HOPE}, a framework with two key components. First, we lift tactile-glove pressure, planar-sensor pressure, and distance-based hand-object contact annotations into a shared hand vertex space, allowing bare-hand contact data to regularize pressure learning where labels are unavailable. Second, we introduce a vertex-anchored video transformer that treats each vertex as a persistent token, aggregates visual features and hand pose over time, and uses a contact-gated pressure head to suppress pressure predictions in regions with low contact probability. Experiments on OpenTouch, PressureVisionDB, and hand-object contact benchmarks validate HOPE across object-pressure, surface-pressure, and contact-supervised HOI settings. Despite using pressure supervision primarily from gloved-hand videos, HOPE generalizes to bare-hand egocentric and in-the-wild videos, producing joint contact and pressure predictions beyond the scope of contact-only or planar-pressure baselines.
  </details>

- **[GUI-Lens: Enabling Precise GUI Grounding for Off-the-Shelf VLMs via Coarse-to-Fine Cropping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03270)**  `arXiv:2608.03270`  
  _Zichuan Fu, Shirong Wang, Wenlin Zhang, Guojing Li, Yimin Deng, Jingtong Gao, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  GUI grounding maps natural-language instructions to click locations and is essential for reliable GUI agents. The task remains difficult on high-resolution, densely populated interfaces because a vision-language model (VLM) may recognize a requested control without locating it precisely enough for interaction. Most existing methods provide various forms of localization assistance, but still rely on a direct click prediction, allowing visual ambiguity or an inaccurate initial estimate to propagate to the final result. In this paper, we introduce GUI-Lens, a coarse-to-fine grounding framework that allows a general-purpose VLM to determine the target through active visual observations. Specifically, GUI-Lens extracts OCR text and detected UI components from the screenshot and presents their positions as coordinate references. Using the instruction, the current view, and these references, the VLM selects the region and scale of the next view, which is cropped and enlarged to provide finer visual details. This process continues over successively focused views until the target is determined. Proposed crops and clicks are checked against the instruction throughout the process, and the final local position is mapped back to the original screen coordinates. Experiments on five GUI grounding benchmarks and six general-purpose base models show that GUI-Lens consistently improves grounding accuracy, achieving up to 9.3 times the accuracy of direct grounding on ScreenSpot-Pro.
  </details>

- **[IR275K: A Benchmark for Infrared Multi-Frame Super-Resolution Toward Efficient Remote Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22380)**  `arXiv:2607.22380`  
  _Jie Deng, Heyang Wang, Changxin Wang, Junkai Shen, Hongyi Chen, Zhiping He, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Infrared multi-frame super-resolution (MFSR) exploits complementary observations to recover spatial detail, but its evaluation remains fragmented across datasets and protocols. A common benchmark is needed to evaluate reconstruction under weak texture, varying thermal contrast, and noise in the source imagery. It should also characterize reconstruction performance across different inter-frame displacement ranges. We introduce IR275K, a curated benchmark containing 594 in- frared video sequences and 275,196 frames. It provides sequence- level train/validation/test splits and a reproducible x4 evaluation protocol. Evaluation spans single-frame and multi-frame recon- struction using seven learned baselines, the CGMamba reference model, and bicubic interpolation. All learned methods are evalu- ated under both bicubic degradation and synthetic mixed sensorthis http URL, whereas MSDTGP leads under mixed sensor noise. DBSR leadsthis http URLablations using CGMamba show that real neighboring frames improve PSNR over center-frame repetition by 1.04 dB under bicubic degradation and 0.95 dB under mixed sensor noise. Center-frame conditioning also improves reconstruction under both settings. The benchmark thus reveals how reconstruction rankings and the benefits of neighboring frames depend on evaluation conditions. The complete IR275K dataset is publicly available at:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[DAUPNet: Domain-Aware Uncertainty Modeling for Reliable Prototype Discrimination in Cross-Domain Few-Shot Semantic Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16308)**  `arXiv:2607.16308`  
  _Lei Yuan, Zhongxu Hu, Jingyi Wen, Pengxing Yi_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Cross-domain few-shot semantic segmentation (CD-FSS) has predominantly been formulated as learning domain-invariant representations or improving support-query correspondence. Nevertheless, large domain shifts still make prototype matching unreliable: inconsistent hierarchical responses corrupt the support representation, deterministic prototypes cannot express boundary and appearance ambiguity, and treating prototypes with different reliability equally during optimization weakens foreground-background separation. We therefore propose DAUPNet, a unified framework that reformulates cross-domain prototype matching as uncertainty-aware prototype discrimination. DAUPNet first harmonizes hierarchical support-query features to provide stable evidence, then represents foreground and background prototypes probabilistically, and finally uses their estimated uncertainty to regulate contrastive optimization. On four standard target domains, DAUPNet achieves 72.6% and 76.7% average mIoU in the 1-shot and 5-shot settings, respectively, including substantial gains on the two medical domains. These results demonstrate that modeling prototype uncertainty and incorporating it into optimization provides a robust and interpretable approach to CD-FSS under severe domain shift. The code is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[NanoVSR: Towards Real-Time Video Super-Resolution on Edge Devices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10495)**  `arXiv:2607.10495`  
  _Filip Pawlicki, Marcel Kańduła, Marcin Pucek, Kamil Dobies_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent Video Super-Resolution (VSR) methods rely heavily on transformers and explicit optical flow, creating computational overhead and custom operations that hinder deployment on hardware accelerators like TensorRT. To address this, we introduce NanoVSR, a scalable, fully convolutional architecture designed for resource-constrained edge devices. Using structural reparameterization, NanoVSR collapses into standard convolutions during inference, ensuring seamless hardware compatibility and negligible runtime overhead. Furthermore, despite lacking explicit motion compensation, it maintains competitive restoration quality by implicitly learning spatio-temporal alignments through progressive training. Evaluated on the REDS4 benchmark, NanoVSR demonstrates an exceptional balance between accuracy and computational efficiency, significantly improving the trade-off for compact architectures. Our NanoVSR-644k baseline yields 28.64 dB PSNR while delivering 27.2 FPS on the NVIDIA Jetson Orin NX 16GB (25W), offering massive speed gains over heavier models. The scaled NanoVSR-1.7M variant reaches 29.15 dB with a throughput of 19.58 FPS, providing superior, edge-optimized upscaling. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Fields of the Planet: Field Boundary Mapping Beyond 10m](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04449)**  `arXiv:2607.04449`  
  _Isaac Corley, Caleb Robinson, Hannah Kerner, Jennifer Marcus_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Field-boundary accuracy depends on the model, sensor resolution, label discretization, and evaluation procedure. Measuring the contribution of sensor resolution therefore requires a controlled comparison across sensors. We present Fields of the Planet (FTP), a 3 m PlanetScope companion to Fields of The World (FTW) containing 133,168 co-registered patch-window targets across 24 countries. FTP preserves FTW's vector polygons, seasonal windows, and dataset splits, enabling direct comparison with 10 m Sentinel-2. We first quantify the resolution ceiling by rasterizing and vectorizing the same ground-truth parcels at each resolution to determine which fields the label grid can represent. We evaluate predicted parcels with polygon-level and boundary metrics. With identical PRUE+ training and post-processing across sensors, plus an upsampled Sentinel-2 control, we isolate gains from image content. PlanetScope raises panoptic quality (PQ) from 21.0 to 35.5, improves PQ on sub-$0.5$ ha fields from 5.8 to 15.7, and reduces matched-boundary error from 18.6 m to 7.4 m. Pixel-level metrics understate this improvement, so field-boundary benchmarks should account for sensor resolution.
  </details>

- **[Soft Mixture-of-Recursions: Going Deeper with Recursive Vision Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00774)**  `arXiv:2607.00774`  
  _Sang In Lee, Jihun Park_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision Transformers are typically scaled by increasing depth or width, at the cost of more unique parameters. We explore recursion as an alternative scaling axis that increases effective computational depth through parameter reuse. Yet simply increasing recursion depth does not reliably improve performance because representations from different recursion stages are not fully utilized. We propose Soft Mixture-of-Recursions (SoftMoR), which learns token-wise weights to combine representations across recursion steps, and instantiate it as the Soft Recursive Vision Transformer (SR-ViT). Experiments on ImageNet-1K, COCO, and ADE20K show that SR-ViT can benefit from deeper recursion while adding few parameters. On ImageNet-1K, increasing recursion depth from T=1 to T=3 improves top-1 accuracy from 79.83% to 82.45% with only 1.1M additional parameters. At matched effective depth, SR-ViT also outperforms independently parameterized counterparts with substantially fewer parameters. These results support recursive deepening as an effective, parameter-efficient scaling axis for Vision Transformers. The code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[PRISM: Feed-Forward Single-Image 3D Reconstruction via Geometric Warp-Residual Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25430)**  `arXiv:2606.25430`  
  _Zhijie Zheng, Xinhao Xiang, Jiawei Zhang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
